The HFLM wrapper gets initialized once, but then every task is evaluated independently with the correct fewshot count for that task. This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   The user (ML research team Worker) needs to set up and run lm-eval-harness benchmarks on server westd-34887 for the distill_sparse_swiglu project's plan_007. The paper currently only has WikiText-2 PPL results and needs downstream accuracy benchmarks (arc_challenge 25-shot, winogrande 5-shot, mmlu 5-shot, gsm8k 5-shot) for three conditions: (1) KL-only sparse (TEAL global 50% sparsity), (2) Dense baseline, (3) Vanilla TEAL baseline. Results should be saved to `/root/distill_sparse_swiglu/results/lm_eval/`, and the registry should be updated with exp_id="lm_eval_kl_s42".

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - lm-eval-harness (v0.4.12) for downstream benchmark evaluation
   - TEAL (Training-free Efficient Activation-based sparsity) with global top-k masks at 50% sparsity
   - PredictorWrapper: monkey-patches LlamaMLP layers with SparsityPredictor (bottleneck MLP) that predicts sparsity masks
   - SwiGLU activation sparsification: gate(x) * up(x) → mask applied before down_proj
   - HFLM wrapper from lm-eval to wrap already-patched models for evaluation
   - Llama-3.1-8B base model in bf16 on NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell (97GB)
   - AutoDL instance requiring `source /etc/network_turbo` for network access and conda environment activation
   - HF_DATASETS_OFFLINE must be set to 0 (overriding script defaults) for dataset downloads

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - `/root/distill_sparse_swiglu/src/eval_benchmark.py`
     - **Most important file**: Comprehensive multi-condition evaluator for plan_007. Handles all 9 conditions (dense, kl_s42, bce_s42, etc.) with both PPL and lm-eval evaluation.
     - Sets `os.environ.setdefault("HF_DATASETS_OFFLINE", "1")` - must override with env var before running
     - `run_lm_eval()` function at line 257: Creates HFLM wrapper, runs each task individually via `lm_eval.simple_evaluate()`
     - `_primary_metric()`: maps task names to metric keys (arc_challenge→acc_norm,none; winogrande→acc,none; mmlu→acc,none; gsm8k→exact_match,strict-match)
     - Args: `--condition dense,kl_s42,teal_vanilla --tasks lm_eval --skip_ppl --gpu 0 --batch_size 4`
     - CONDITIONS dict defines all conditions with their modes and checkpoint paths
     - LM_EVAL_TASKS: OrderedDict with arc_challenge(25), winogrande(5), mmlu(5), gsm8k(5)
     
   - `/root/distill_sparse_swiglu/src/benchmark_eval.py`
     - Simpler per-condition benchmark script. Uses `run_lm_eval_tasks()` at line 225.
     - Does NOT set offline mode flags.
     - LM_EVAL_DEFAULTS: arc_challenge(25), winogrande(5), mmlu(5), gsm8k(8-shot, differs from eval_benchmark)
     
   - `/root/distill_sparse_swiglu/src/predictor.py`
     - SparsityPredictor: bottleneck MLP (hidden→128→intermediate) predicting mask logits
     - PredictorWrapper: monkey-patches model.model.layers[i].mlp.forward with predictor-based masking
     - GumbelSigmoidMask: differentiable binary mask for training, hard threshold for inference
     - CompensationNetwork: per-layer correction heads (FastForward-style)
     
   - `/root/distill_sparse_swiglu/src/evaluate.py`
     - evaluate_perplexity(): takes model + optional global_masks, monkey-patches MLPs with static masks
     - evaluate_true_dense(): restores original MLP forwards, evaluates unsparsified model
     - teal_global_masks(): predictor-based global ranking for TEAL mask computation
     - Model default: `--model_name_or_path /root/autodl-tmp/models/llama-3.1-8b`
     
   - `/root/distill_sparse_swiglu/setup_env.sh`
     - Sources `/etc/network_turbo`, sets HF_HOME=/root/autodl-tmp/.hf_cache, HF_HUB_DISABLE_XET=1, SSL certs, activates conda base
     
   - `/root/distill_sparse_swiglu/results/plan_007/consolidated/results.json`
     - Contains existing WikiText-2 PPL results for all 9 conditions (only PPL, no downstream)
     
   - `/root/distill_sparse_swiglu/checkpoints/mvp_kl_norm_v2_s42/predictor_kl_normalized.pt`
     - KL-only predictor checkpoint (seed 42) - verified to exist

4. Errors and fixes:

The loglikelihood requests are running at ~3.77 it/s for the winogrande task. This is much slower than arc_challenge's 46 it/s. The reason is probably that winogrande has longer prompts (with 5-shot examples). 

At 3.77 it/s for 4687 requests, that's 4687/3.77 ≈ 1243 seconds ≈ 20 minutes for this task alone. arc_challenge was 25 seconds. 

Wait - 4687 requests for winogrande? That can't be right. 

Winogrande should only have 1267 test samples × 2 choices = 2534 loglikelihood calls, so either the request count includes multiple tasks batched together, or I'm misidentifying which task is actually running. Looking at the eval_benchmark.py script, it runs each task individually through `simple_evaluate`, so the 4687 requests shouldn't be from winogrande alone. The script processes tasks sequentially—arc_challenge, then winogrande, then mmlu, then gsm8k—and arc_challenge had 1172 requests, so 4687 doesn't match winogrande's expected count. Maybe the script is doing something different than just loglikelihood requests per sample.

The real issue is the speed difference: this task is running at 3.77 it/s compared to 46 it/s for arc_challenge, likely because of the longer context with 5-shot examples and more verbose prompts. At this rate, 4687 requests will take roughly 20 minutes. Looking at the full pipeline, arc_challenge finished in about 25 seconds, but MMLU could have 14,000+ requests across 57 subjects at similar speeds, pushing the total runtime to over an hour, and that's before GSM8K.

With all three conditions, I'm looking at 4.5-6 hours total, which fits within the 6-hour timeout. Since the job seems stable now, my plan is to let dense finish (estimated 1-2 hours), then queue up kl_s42 and teal_vanilla sequentially. I'll check back in about 20 minutes to see how much progress we've made.

Let me estimate the total time for dense more carefully:
- arc_challenge (done): ~25s
- winogrande: 4687 requests at ~5.5 it/s ≈ 14 min remaining


- MMLU is going to be the bottleneck with ~14k test examples (56k requests total), which at 5-6 it/s translates to roughly 2.6 hours. GSM8K uses a different evaluation approach with text generation rather than loglikelihood scoring, so that'll be slower too. This whole evaluation is going to take several hours.

I could increase the batch size since the GPU has 98GB available and we're only using about 20GB, but I've already submitted the job with batch_size=4 and it's running. Killing it now would waste the progress on arc_challenge. I'll just let it run and monitor the progress—winogrande should finish in about 13 minutes at the current rate.

I'm curious how MMLU will perform since it might expand into many subtasks. Let me check back in 15 minutes to see how things are progressing.
